{\bf K}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}=&{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{1}&+&{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{1}&+&{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{2}{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{2}&+&{\bf x}_{1}{\bf x}_{2}{\bf x}_{2}{\bf x}_{2}&+\cdots.\end{array}

Lemma 6.2
.

Given the labelled edge-coloured digraph
{\bf G}
associated to
(P,\gamma,\sigma)
, we have

{\mathbf{K}}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}={\mathscr{F}}_{\bf G}.

6.2.
The analogue of the fundamental theorem

Proposition 6.3
.

We have that

\mathbf{K}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}=\sum_{w\in\mathcal{L}(P)}\mathbf{K}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.7
, we have
\mathcal{B}(P,\gamma)=\bigsqcup_{w\in\mathcal{L}(P)}\mathcal{B}(w,\gamma)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}=\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}(P,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}=\sum_{w\in\mathcal{L}(P)}\left(\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}(w,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}\right)=\sum_{w\in\mathcal{L}(P)}\mathbf{K}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}.

∎

6.3.
Enriched fundamental functions to monomial basis

First, we expand the
\mathbf{K}
functions in the
\mathbf{M}
basis, showing that
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is a subspace of
\mathrm{NCQSym}
. The idea of the proof is similar to the commutative case given in
[
48
]
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Assume
w=(w_{1},w_{2},\dots,w_{n})
is a chain and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, we have

\mathbf{K}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}=\sum_{A\subseteq[n-1]\atop\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)\subseteq A\cup(A+1)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}.

Proof.

Recall that

{\mathbf{K}}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}=\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}(w,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}.

Fix
A=\{a_{1}<a_{2}<\dots<a_{k}\}\subseteq[n-1]
with
0=a_{0}
and
a_{k+1}=n
. Define

[A]=12\dots a_{1}~|~(a_{1}+1)(a_{1}+2)\dots a_{2}~|\cdots|(a_{k}+1)(a_{k}+2)\cdots a_{n}.

Let

\mathcal{B}_{A}(w,\gamma)

be the set of all
f\in\mathcal{B}(w,\gamma)
such that

(1)

|f(w_{i})|=|f(w_{j})|
if
i
and
j
are in the same block of
[A]
.

(2)

|f(w_{i})|<|f(w_{j})|
if
i
is in a block of
[A]
to the left of the block containing
j
.

Then

{\mathbf{K}}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}=\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}(w,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}=\sum_{A\subseteq[n-1]}\left(\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}_{A}(w,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}\right).

We will show the following claims are true.

(I)

If
\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)\not\subseteq A\cup(A+1)
, then
{\mathcal{B}}_{A}(w,\gamma)
is empty.

(II)

If
\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)\subseteq A\cup(A+1)
, then

\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}_{A}(w,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}=2^{|A|+1}{\mathbf{M}}_{(A,\sigma)}.

Therefore,

{\mathbf{K}}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}=\sum_{A\subseteq[n-1]}\left(\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}_{A}(w,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}\right)=\sum_{A\subseteq[n-1]\atop\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)\subseteq A\cup(A+1)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)},

as desired.

Proof of
(I). Let
b\in\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)\setminus A\cup(A+1)
. Since

\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)\setminus A\cup(A+1)\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}\setminus A\cup(A+1)=

\{2,3,\dots,a_{1}-1,a_{1}+2,a_{1}+3,\dots,a_{2}-1,\dots,a_{k}+2,a_{k}+3,\dots,n\},

we have that
b-1,b,b+1
are in the same block of
[A]
. Therefore, if
f\in{\mathcal{B}}_{A}(w,\gamma)\neq\emptyset
, we must have

|f(w_{b-1})|=|f(w_{b})|=|f(w_{b+1})|.

Note that
b
is in
\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)
, and so

\gamma(w_{b-1})<\gamma(w_{b})>\gamma(w_{b+1}).

Since
f
is an enriched
(P,\gamma)
-partition,

f(w_{b-1})\preceq f(w_{b})\preceq f(w_{b+1}).

If
|f(w_{b-1})|=|f(w_{b})|
then
f(w_{b-1})=f(b)>0
, and if
|f(w_{b})|=|f(w_{b+1})|
then
f(w_{b-1})=f(b)<0
. Therefore, we can not have
|f(w_{b-1})|=|f(w_{b})|=|f(w_{b+1})|,
a contradiction.
Therefore,
{\mathcal{B}}_{A}(w,\gamma)=\emptyset
.

Proof of
(II). If
\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)\subseteq A\cup(A+1)
, then for each
1\leq j\leq\ell-1
, we have

\gamma(w_{a_{j}+1})\leq\gamma(w_{a_{j}+2})\leq\dots\leq\gamma(w_{a_{j}+t})\geq\dots\geq\gamma(w_{a_{j+1}-1})\geq\gamma(w_{a_{j+1}})

(note that we may have
t=1
or
t=(a_{j+1}-a_{j})
, which in these cases
\gamma
is decreasing or increasing, respectively).
Then there are two types of
f\in{\mathcal{B}}_{A}(P,\gamma)
:

•

f(w_{a_{j}+t})<0
,
f(w_{a_{j}+t})=f(w_{a_{j}+s})
for
1\leq s\leq t
, and
f(w_{a_{j}+t})=-f(w_{a_{j}+s})
for
t+1\leq s\leq a_{j+1}-a_{j}
.

•

f(w_{a_{j}+t})>0
,
f(w_{a_{j}+t})=f(w_{a_{j}+s})
for
1\leq s\leq t
, and
f(w_{a_{j}+t})=-f(w_{a_{j}+s})
for
t+1\leq s\leq a_{j+1}-a_{j}
.

Therefore, there are
2^{|A|+1}
possible choices of
f\in{\mathcal{B}}_{A}(P,\gamma)
such that they produce the monomial

{\bf x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(1))|}{\bf x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(2))|}\dots{\bf x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(n))|}

such that

(1)

|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|=|f(\sigma^{-1}(j))|
if
i
and
j
are in the same block of
[A]
.

(2)

|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|<|f(\sigma^{-1}(j))|
if
i
is in a block of
[A]
to the left of the block containing
j
.

Therefore,

\sum_{f\in\mathcal{B}_{A}(w,\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{|f(\sigma^{-1}(i))|}=2^{|A|+1}{\mathbf{M}}_{(A,\sigma)},

as desired.
∎

Corollary 6.5
.

We have that

\mathbf{K}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}=\mathbf{K}_{(w,\gamma^{\prime},\sigma)}
if and only if
\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)=\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma^{\prime})
.

Given a peak set
B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, define

\mathbf{K}_{(B,\sigma)}=\mathbf{K}_{(w,\gamma,\sigma)}

where
(w,\gamma)
is any labelled chain with
\mathrm{Peak}(w,\gamma)=B
.

7.
Peak algebra in noncommuting variables and its dimension

Definition 7.1
.

Let
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
be the space spanned by all enriched fundamental functions in noncommuting variables
{\mathbf{K}}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}
, or equivalently, the space spanned by the following set

\{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bf G}:{\bf G}\text{~is a labelled edge-coloured digraph}\}.

The space
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is called the
peak algebra in noncommuting variables
.

7.1.
Enriched monomial functions

Given
(B,\sigma)
where
B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}
is a peak set and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, define the
enriched monomial function in noncommuting variables

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}
to be

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}=\sum_{C\subseteq B}(-1)^{|B|-|C|}\mathbf{K}_{(C,\sigma)}.

By inclusion-exclusion, we have

\mathbf{K}_{(B,\sigma)}=\sum_{C\subseteq B}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(C,\sigma)}.

Corollary 7.2
.

Both sets

\{{\mathbf{K}}_{(B,\sigma)}:\text{~$B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}$ is a peak set, $\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}$}\}

and

\{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}:\text{~$B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}$ is a peak set, $\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}$}\}

span
\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}
.

Proof.

By definition

\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is spanned by
\{{\mathbf{K}}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}\}
, and
by Corollary
6.5
, if
\mathrm{Peak}(P,\gamma)=\mathrm{Peak}(P,\gamma^{\prime})
, then
{\mathbf{K}}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}={\mathbf{K}}_{(P,\gamma^{\prime},\sigma)}
. Therefore, the set

\{{\mathbf{K}}_{(B,\sigma)}:\text{~$B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}$ is a peak set, $\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}$}\}

spans
\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}
, and by triangulation we have

\{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}:\text{~$B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}$ is a peak set, $\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}$}\}

spans
\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}
.

∎

7.2.
Enriched monomial functions to monomials

In order to show that the dimension of
\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}
is the same as the number of odd set compositions of
[n]
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 7.3
.

For any peak set
B\subseteq[n-1]
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, we have

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}=(-1)^{|B|}\sum_{A\subseteq\mathrm{Odd}(B)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
6.4
that

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{C\subseteq B}(-1)^{|B|-|C|}\sum_{A\subseteq[n-1]\atop C\subseteq A\cup(A+1)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{A\subseteq[n-1]}\left(\sum_{C\subseteq(A\cup(A+1))\cap B}(-1)^{|B|-|C|}\right)2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|B|}\sum_{A\subseteq[n-1]\setminus(B\cup(B-1))}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}.

The last equality follows from the observation that the sum inside the bracket vanishes unless
(A\cup(A+1))\cap B=\emptyset
which is equivalent to
A\subseteq[n-1]\setminus(B\cup(B-1))
. Note that
\mathrm{Odd}(B)=[n-1]\setminus(B\cup(B-1))
, thus

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}=(-1)^{|B|}\sum_{A\subseteq\mathrm{Odd}(B)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}.

∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
B\subseteq[n-1]
be a peak set and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
. Let
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
. If

\mathrm{Std}(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma)=\mathrm{Std}(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma^{\prime}),

then

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma^{\prime})}.

Proof.

Note that

{\mathbf{M}}_{(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma)}={\mathbf{M}}_{(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma^{\prime})}.

Thus, for any
A\subseteq\mathrm{Odd}(B)
,

{\mathbf{M}}_{(A,\sigma)}={\mathbf{M}}_{(A,\sigma^{\prime})}.

Therefore,

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}=(-1)^{|B|}\sum_{A\subseteq\mathrm{Odd}(B)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}=(-1)^{|B|}\sum_{A\subseteq\mathrm{Odd}(B)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma^{\prime})}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma^{\prime})}.

∎

7.3.
Bases of the peak algebra in noncommuting variables and its dimension

Recall that if
B\subseteq[n-1]
is a peak set and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, we say
(B,\sigma)
is enriched standard if
(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma)
is standard.

Our main result of this section is the following.

Theorem 7.5
.

The sets

\{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}:B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\},\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n},(B,\sigma)\text{~is enriched standard}\}

and

\{{\mathbf{K}}_{(B,\sigma)}:B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\},\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n},(B,\sigma)\text{~is enriched standard}\}

are bases for
\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}.
Moreover,

\dim(\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n})=|\{\phi\vDash[n]:\phi\text{~is odd}\}|.

Proof.

Given any pair
(B,\sigma)
where
B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\}
is a peak set and
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
, let
\sigma^{\prime}
be the unique permutation in
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
such that

\mathrm{Std}(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma)=(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma^{\prime}).

By Lemma
7.4
,

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma^{\prime})}.

Moreover,

\{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}:B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\},\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n},(B,\sigma)\text{~is enriched standard}\}

is linearly independent by triangularity of the transition matrix from
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
to
{\mathbf{M}}
. Therefore,
this set is a basis for
\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}
.

Observe that if
(B,\sigma)
is enriched standard, then for any
C\subseteq B
,
(C,\sigma)
is also enriched standard. Therefore,

\{{\mathbf{K}}_{(B,\sigma)}:B\subseteq\{2,3,\dots,n-1\},\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n},(B,\sigma)\text{~is enriched standard}\}

also forms a basis for
\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}
.
∎

8.
Hopf structure of the peak algebra in noncommuting variables

The goal of this section is to prove that
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is a Hopf subalgebra of
\mathrm{NCQSym}
.

Recall that the product in
\mathrm{NCQSym}
is the regular product of power series. The coproduct in
\mathrm{NCQSym}
is defined as follows (see
[
12
]
). By introducing another set of noncommuting variables
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2},\dots
with linear order
\mathbf{x}_{1}<\mathbf{x}_{2}<\cdots<\mathbf{y}_{1}<\mathbf{y}_{2}<\cdots
, we have the following map composition

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x})&\cong&\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})&\to&\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\sim&\to&\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{y})\\
f(\mathbf{x})&\mapsto&f(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})&\mapsto&\bar{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})&\mapsto&\sum\bar{f_{1}}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\bar{f_{2}}(\mathbf{y})\end{array}

where
\sim
denotes the relations
\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{j}=\mathbf{y}_{j}\mathbf{x}_{i}
for all
i,j
. Then, the coproduct is given by

\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\Delta:&\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x})&\to&\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{y})&\cong&\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathrm{NCQSym}(\mathbf{x})\\
&f(\mathbf{x})&\mapsto&\sum\bar{f_{1}}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\bar{f_{2}}(\mathbf{y})&\mapsto&\sum\bar{f_{1}}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\bar{f_{2}}(\mathbf{x})\end{array}

Let
\bf G
be a labelled edge-coloured digraph, and let
n
be a positive integer. Then
{\bf G}+n
is the labelled edge-coloured digraph obtained by replacing each vertex
a
of
\bf G
by
a+n
.

Given labelled edge-coloured digraphs
{\bf G}
and
{\bf H}
, by
[
3
, Proposition 10.1]

{\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf G}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf H}={\mathscr{Y}}_{{\bf G}|{\bf H}}

where
{\bf G}|{\bf H}
is the digraph of disjoint union of
{\bf G}
and
{\bf H}+|V({\bf G})|
.

\bf 1
\bf 2
\bf 3
Q_{\{1,2,3\}}|Q_{\{1,2,3\}}
{\bf 4}
\bf 5
\bf 6

Let
\bf G
be a labelled edge-coloured digraph. The
standardization
of
\bf G
, denoted
\mathrm{Std}({\bf G})
, is the labelled edge-coloured digraph obtained by replacing the
i
th smallest element of
V({\bf G})
by
i
.
For a subset
A
of
V({\bf G})
, let
{\bf G}|_{A}
be the induced labelled edge-coloured subdigraph of
{\bf G}
with vertex set
A
.
An edge-coloured subdigraph
{\bf F}
of
{\bf G}
is called a
\{\rightarrow,\Rightarrow\}
-induced subdigraph
of
{\bf G}

if
{\bf F}
is an induced subdigraph of
{\bf G}
and if
a\in V({\bf F})
and either
a\rightarrow b
or
a\Rightarrow b
in
{\bf G}
, then
b\in V({\bf F})
. Then by
[
3
, Proposition 10.2]

\Delta({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf G})=\sum_{{\bf F}}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mathrm{Std}({\bf G}|_{V({\bf G})\setminus V({\bf F})})}\otimes{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mathrm{Std}({\bf F})}

where the sum is over all
\{\rightarrow,\Rightarrow\}
-induced subdigraphs
{\bf F}
of
{\bf G}
.

With a similar proof for the product and coproduct of enriched generalized chromatic functions. We have

{\mathscr{F}}_{\bf G}{\mathscr{F}}_{\bf H}={\mathscr{F}}_{{\bf G}|{\bf H}},

and

\Delta({\mathscr{F}}_{\bf G})=\sum_{{\bf F}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathrm{Std}({\bf G}|_{V({\bf G})\setminus V({\bf F})})}\otimes{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathrm{Std}({\bf F})}

where the sum is over all
\{\rightarrow,\Rightarrow\}
-induced subdigraphs
{\bf F}
of
{\bf G}
.

Therefore
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is isomorphic to a Hopf subalgebra of
\mathrm{NCQSym}
.

9.
The labelled descent-to-peak map

The
labelled descent-to-peak map
from
\mathrm{NCQSym}
to
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
, denoted
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}
, is the linear map

(9.1)

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}:&\mathrm{NCQSym}&\to&\mathrm{NC}\Pi\\
&\mathbf{F}_{(A,\sigma)}&\mapsto&\mathbf{K}_{(A\setminus((A+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}\end{array}

for all standard pairs
(A,\sigma)
.

Let
A=\{a_{1}<a_{2}<\dots<a_{k}\}\subseteq[n-1]
with
n-a_{k}
is odd. We define

\mathrm{ODiff}(A)=\{a_{i}:1\leq i\leq k,a_{i}-a_{i-1}\text{ is odd}\}

where
a_{0}=0
. For example, if
A=\{1,3,4,8\}\subseteq[9]
, then
\mathrm{ODiff}(A)=\{1,4\}
.

Remark that
\mathrm{ODiff}(A)
is an odd set, and there is a unique peak set
B
such that

\mathrm{Odd}(B)=\mathrm{ODiff}(A).

The following lemma will be useful in the study of the labelled descent-to-peak map.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
A\subseteq[n-1]
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
. Then

\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}=\begin{cases}(-1)^{n-1-|B|}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}&\text{ if }n-\max(A)\text{ is odd,}\\
0&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

where
B
is the unique peak set such that
\mathrm{Odd}(B)=\mathrm{ODiff}(A)
.

Proof.

Consider the set
\{C\subseteq[n-1]:A\subseteq C\}
. Let the sign of
C
to be
(-1)^{|C|}
.

We first show that if
n-\max(A)
is even, then

\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}=0

using a sign reversing involution
\imath
with no fixed point defined as follows.

Let
C_{\geq\max(A)}
be the set of the elements of
C
that are bigger than or equal to
\max(A)
. Let

(C_{\geq\max(A)}\setminus\{1\})\cup\{\max(A),n+1\}=\{\max(A)=d_{1}<d_{2}<\cdots<d_{k}=n+1\}.

The set
\{1\leq i\leq k-1:d_{i+1}-d_{i}\text{~is odd}\}
is non-empty since
d_{k}-d_{1}=n+1-\max(A)
is odd. Let
j=\min(\{1\leq i\leq k-1:d_{i+1}-d_{i}\text{~is odd}\})
; that is
j
is the smallest integer such that
d_{j+1}-d_{j}
is odd. If
C\cap\{d_{j}+1,d_{j}+2,\dots,d_{j+1}\}=\emptyset
, set
m_{C}=0
, otherwise, let
m_{C}
be the largest integer such that

d_{j}+1,d_{j}+2,\ldots,d_{j}+m_{C}\in C\cap\{d_{j}+1,d_{j}+2,\dots,d_{j+1}\}.

Now define

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\imath:&\{C\subseteq[n-1]:A\subseteq C\}&\rightarrow&\{C\subseteq[n-1]:A\subseteq C\}\\
&C&\mapsto&\begin{cases}C\setminus\{d_{j}+m_{C}\}&\text{if ~}m_{C}\text{~is odd,}\\
C\cup\{d_{j}+m_{C}+1\}&\text{if ~}m_{C}\text{~is even.}\end{cases}\end{array}

Then
\imath
is a sign reversing involution with no fixed point. Moreover, for any
C
, we have

\imath(C)\setminus((\imath(C)+1)\cup\{1\})=C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),

and so

\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}=\mathbf{K}_{\imath(C)\setminus((\imath(C)+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}.

Consequently,

\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}=0.

Let
n-\max(A)
be odd and let
B
be the unique peak set such that
\mathrm{Odd}(B)=\mathrm{ODiff}(A)
. We want to show that

\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}=(-1)^{n-1-|B|}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}

using a sing reversing involution
\jmath
defined as follows.

Define

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\jmath:&\{C\subseteq[n-1]:A\subseteq C\}&\rightarrow&\{C\subseteq[n-1]:A\subseteq C\}\\
&C&\mapsto&\begin{cases}C&\text{if~}B\subseteq C,\\
C\setminus\{\ (\min(B)\setminus C)-1\}&\text{if~}B\not\subseteq C,(\min(B)\setminus C)-1\in C,\\
C\cup\{\ (\min(B)\setminus C)-1\}&\text{if~}B\not\subseteq C,(\min(B)\setminus C)-1\not\in C.\\
\end{cases}\end{array}

Note that the set of fixed points of
\jmath
is

\{C\subseteq[n-1]:A,B\subseteq C\}.

We have that

\{C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}):A,B\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]\}=\{D:D\subseteq B\}.

The reason for this equality is that if
A,B\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]
, then

C=[n-1]\setminus\{d-1:d\in D\}

for some
D\subseteq B
.
Note that
|D|=n-1-|C|
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{A,B\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{D\subseteq B}(-1)^{n-1-|D|}\mathbf{K}_{(D,\sigma)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n-1-|B|}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}.

∎

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
A\subseteq[n-1]
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
.
Let
(A,\sigma)
be standard such that
n-\max(A)
is odd. Then

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)})=\begin{cases}(-1)^{n-1-|B|-|A|}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}&\text{ if }n-\max(A)\text{ is odd,}\\
0&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

where
B
is the unique peak set such that
\mathrm{Odd}(B)=\mathrm{ODiff}(A)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.8
and inclusion-exclusion, we have

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)})=\sum_{A\subseteq C}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{F}_{(C,\sigma)}).

For any
A\subseteq C
, we must have
(C,\sigma)
is standard. Hence by definition,
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{F}_{(C,\sigma)})=\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}
. Let
B
be the unique peak set such that
\mathrm{Odd}(B)=\mathrm{ODiff}(A)
. Then, by Lemma
9.1
, we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(-1)^{n-1-|B|-|A|}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}&\text{ if }n-\max(A)\text{ is odd,}\\
0&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

∎

Remark 9.3
.

A commutative version of Proposition
9.2
appears in
[
27
, Theorem 2.4]
. So this result is an extension of
[
27
, Theorem 2.4]
.

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
(P,\gamma)
be a labelled poset with ground set
X
and
|X|=n
. Let
\sigma:X\to[n]
be a bijection.
We have that:

(1)

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{F}_{(A,\sigma)})=\mathbf{K}_{(A\setminus((A+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}
for all
A\subseteq[n-1]
,
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
(
(A,\sigma)
is not necessarily standard).

(2)

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{F}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)})=\mathbf{K}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}
.

(3)

The following diagram commutes.
\mathrm{NCQSym}
\mathrm{QSym}
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
\Pi
\rho
\rho
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}
\Theta_{\mathrm{QSym}}

Proof.

Recall that

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}
is the linear map

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}:&\mathrm{NCQSym}&\to&\mathrm{NC}\Pi\\
&\mathbf{F}_{(A,\sigma)}&\mapsto&\mathbf{K}_{(A\setminus((A+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}\end{array}

where
(A,\sigma)
is standard.

(1) We prove by induction on
|A|
. If
|A|=n-1
, then
(A,\sigma)
must be standard and we are done.

Assume the statement is true for all
n-1\geq|A|>k
. We want to show that for any
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
and
A\subseteq[n-1]
with
|A|=k
,
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{F}_{(A,\sigma)})=\mathbf{K}_{(A\setminus((A+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}
.
Let
\mathrm{std}(A,\sigma)=(A,\sigma^{\prime})
. By inclusion-exclusion, we have

\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{F}_{(C,\sigma)}=\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}=\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma^{\prime})}=\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{F}_{(C,\sigma^{\prime})}.

Let
B
be the peak set such that
\mathrm{Odd}(B)=\mathrm{ODiff}(A)
. Since
\mathrm{ODiff}(A)\subseteq A
, we have
\mathrm{Std}(\mathrm{Odd}(A),\sigma)=\mathrm{Std}(\mathrm{Odd}(A),\sigma^{\prime})
. Therefore,
\mathrm{Std}(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma)=\mathrm{Std}(\mathrm{Odd}(B),\sigma^{\prime})
, and so by Lemma
7.4
, we have
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma^{\prime})}
. Then, by Lemma
9.1
,

\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}=\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma^{\prime})}.

Therefore, by induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{F}_{(A,\sigma)})

\displaystyle=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}\left(\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{F}_{(C,\sigma^{\prime})}-\sum_{A\subsetneq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{F}_{(C,\sigma)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{A\subseteq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma^{\prime})}-\sum_{A\subsetneq C\subseteq[n-1]}(-1)^{|C|-|A|}\mathbf{K}_{(C\setminus((C+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{K}_{(A\setminus((A+1)\cup\{1\}),\sigma)}

as desired.

Part (2) follows from (1) by Propositions
5.5
and
6.3
, and (3) follows from the fact that the commutation map
\rho
sends
\mathbf{F}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}
and
\mathbf{K}_{(P,\gamma,\sigma)}
to
F_{(P,\gamma)}
and
K_{(P,\gamma)}
, respectively.
∎

Corollary 9.5
.

We have that

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}:&\mathrm{NCQSym}&\rightarrow&\mathrm{NC}\Pi\\
&{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mathbf{G}}&\mapsto&{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathbf{G}}.\end{array}

Moreover, the following diagram commutes.

\mathsf{\mathrm{NCQSym}}
\mathrm{QSym}
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
\Pi
\rho
\rho
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}
\Theta_{\mathrm{QSym}}
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mathbf{G}}
{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathbf{G}}
{\mathscr{X}}_{G}
{\mathscr{E}}_{G}

Comparing the product and coproduct formulas for generalized chromatic functions and enriched generalized chromatic functions in noncommuting variables in Section
8
yield the following result.

Theorem 9.6
.

The labelled descent-to-peak map
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}
is a Hopf morphism. Hence,
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is both a Hopf subalgebra and a quotient of
\mathrm{NCQSym}
.

10.
Schur
Q
-functions in noncommuting variables

In this section we define the space of Schur
Q
-functions in noncommuting variables, which is a Hopf subalgebra of symmetric functions in noncommuting variables
\mathrm{NCSym}
. We will follow notation from
[
40
]
.

10.1.
Symmetric functions in noncommuting variables

For any set composition
\phi
, define
\pi(\phi)
to be the set partition with the same blacks as
\phi
. For example,

\pi(256|14|389)=256/389/14.

Given two set partitions
\pi=\pi_{1}/\pi_{2}/\cdots/\pi_{\ell(\pi)}\vdash[n]
and
\tau=\tau_{1}/\tau_{2}/\cdots/\tau_{\ell(\tau)}\vdash[m]
, the
shifted concatenation
of
\pi
and
\tau
is

\pi|\tau=\pi_{1}/\pi_{2}/\cdots/\pi_{\ell(\pi)}/\tau_{1}+n/\tau_{2}+n/\cdots/\tau_{\ell(\tau)}+n.

Recall that
\{\pi\vdash[n]\}
forms a lattice defined as
\pi\leq\tau
if all blocks of
\tau
is contained in some block of
\pi
.
Throughout this section, we use
\mu(\pi,\tau)
to denote the Möbius function of
\pi
and
\tau
in this lattice.
Let
\hat{0}
and
\hat{1}
denote the minimal element
12\cdots n
and the maximal element
1/2/\cdots/n
, respectively. It is well-known that
\mu(\hat{0},\pi)=\prod_{i}(-1)^{\lambda_{i}-1}(\lambda_{i}-1)!
where
(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\ell})=\lambda(\pi)
.

For any set partition
\pi
, define

{\mathbf{m}}_{\pi}=\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\pi)=\pi}{\mathbf{M}}_{\phi}.

Then the
Hopf algebra of symmetric functions in noncommuting variables
is

\mathrm{NCSym}=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{NCSym}_{n}

where

\mathrm{NCSym}_{n}=\mathbb{Q}\text{-span}\{{\mathbf{m}}_{\pi}:\pi\vdash[n]\}.

The set
\{{\mathbf{m}}_{\pi}\}
is called the
monomial basis
of
\mathrm{NCSym}
. Moreover, define

{\mathbf{p}}_{\pi}=\sum_{\tau\leq\pi}{\mathbf{m}}_{\tau}.

The set
\{{\mathbf{p}}_{\pi}\}
is a basis for
\mathrm{NCSym}
and it is called the
power sum basis
of
\mathrm{NCSym}
.

There is also a fundamental involution

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\omega:&\mathrm{NCSym}&\to&\mathrm{NCSym}\\
&{\mathbf{h}}_{\pi}&\mapsto&{\mathbf{e}}_{\pi}.\end{array}

Moreover,

\omega({\mathbf{p}}_{\pi})=(-1)^{n-\ell(\pi)}{\mathbf{p}}_{\pi}.

We will also use the following result.

Theorem 10.1
.

[
40
, Theorems 3.4 and 3.5]

For any set partition
\pi
, we have the following.

(1)

\mathbf{e}_{\pi}=\displaystyle\sum_{\tau\leq\pi}\mu(\hat{0},\tau)\mathbf{p}_{\tau}
,

(2)

\mathbf{h}_{\pi}=\displaystyle\sum_{\tau\leq\pi}|\mu(\hat{0},\tau)|\mathbf{p}_{\tau}
.

10.2.
Theta map for symmetric functions in noncommuting variables

Define

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}|_{\mathrm{NCSym}}.

Theorem 10.2
.

Suppose
\pi=\pi_{1}/\pi_{2}/\cdots/\pi_{\ell(\pi)}\vdash[n]
is a set partition with
n\geq 1
, then

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}(\mathbf{p}_{\pi})=\begin{cases}2^{\ell(\pi)}\mathbf{p}_{\pi}&\text{ if }\pi\text{ is odd set partition,}\\
0&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Consequently,
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}
commutes with
\omega
, that is, the following diagram is commutative.
\mathrm{NCSym}
\mathrm{NCSym}
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}
\omega
\omega

To prove the above theorem, first we need the following lemma. For set compositions
\phi
and
\psi
, define
\psi\leq\phi
if each part of
\psi
is a union of consecutive parts of
\phi
. Let
\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)
be a set composition that union the maximal segment whose the sizes of the blocks are maximal segments of the form
({\rm even},\dots,{\rm even},{\rm odd})
(for example,
\mathrm{Odd}(1|58|69|4|23|7)=1|45689|237
).

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\pi
be an odd set partition. For any
\psi=\psi_{1}|\psi_{2}|\cdots|\psi_{\ell(\psi)}
with
\pi(\psi)\leq\pi
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop{\pi(\phi)\leq\pi,\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)\geq\psi\atop|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|\text{is odd}}}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}=2^{\ell(\pi)-\ell(\psi)}(-1)^{\ell(\pi)}.

Proof.

We call
\phi\vDash[n]

suitable
if it appears in the above summation, that is,
\pi(\phi)\leq\pi,\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)\geq\psi
, and
|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|
. Consider that
\phi\vDash[n]
is suitable if and only if
\phi=\phi_{1}|\phi_{2}|\cdots|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}\leq\pi
such that for all
j
,
\psi_{j}=\phi_{i_{j-1}+1}\cup\cdots\cup\phi_{i_{j}}
for some
i_{j}
, and
|\phi_{i_{j}}|
is odd. In particular,
i_{0}=0
and
i_{\ell(\psi)}=\ell(\phi)
.

With the notation above, let
I
be the collection of all possible tuples
(\phi_{i_{j}}:1\leq j\leq\ell(\psi))
, that is,

I=\{(\phi_{i_{1}},\phi_{i_{2}},\dots,\phi_{i_{\ell(\psi)}}):\pi(\phi)\leq\pi,\text{ for all ~}j,\psi_{j}=\phi_{i_{j-1}+1}\cup\cdots\cup\phi_{i_{j}}\text{ and }|\phi_{i_{j}}|\text{ is odd}\}.

For each
D=(D_{1},\dots,D_{\ell(\psi)})\in I
, let
I_{D}
be the collection of all suitable
\phi
with the given tuple
D
, that is,

I_{D}=\{\phi\vDash[n]:\pi(\phi)\leq\pi\text{~ and for all ~}j,\psi_{j}=\phi_{i_{j-1}+1}\cup\cdots\cup\phi_{i_{j}}\text{ with }\phi_{i_{j}}=D_{j}\}.

Then, we have

\sum_{\phi\in Q_{\pi},\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)\geq\phi^{\prime}\atop\left|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}\right|\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}=\sum_{D\in I}\sum_{\phi\in I_{D}}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}.

Fix
D=(D_{1},\dots,D_{\ell(\psi)})\in I
. For each
j
, let
\psi_{j}\setminus D_{j}
be the union of
a_{j}
blocks of
\pi
. Let
D_{j}
be the union of
b_{j}
blocks of
\pi
. Since all blocks of
\pi
are odd and
|D_{j}|
is odd, we must have
b_{j}
is odd. Also,

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}(a_{j}+b_{j})=\ell(\pi).

Note that
I_{D}
consists of all
\phi\vDash[n]
such that
\psi_{j}\setminus D_{j}=\phi_{i_{j-1}+1}\cup\cdots\cup\phi_{i_{j}-1}
, and each
\phi_{p}\subseteq\psi_{j}\setminus D_{j}
is a union of some of the
a_{j}
blocks of
\pi
. Therefore, each tuple of blocks
(\phi_{i_{j-1}+1},\dots,\phi_{i_{j}-1})
corresponds to a set composition
\varphi
of
[a_{j}]
. Thus, we have

\sum_{\phi\in I_{D}}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}\sum_{\varphi\models[a_{j}]}(-1)^{\ell(\varphi)+1}=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}(-1)^{a_{j}+1}=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}(-1)^{a_{j}+b_{j}}=(-1)^{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}a_{j}+b_{j}}=(-1)^{\ell(\pi)},

where
the second equality follows from the well-known fact that
\sum_{\varphi\vDash[a]}(-1)^{\ell(\varphi)}=(-1)^{a}
for all
a
.

Suppose
\psi_{j}
is a union of
c_{j}
blocks of
\pi
with
\sum_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}c_{j}=\ell(\pi)
. Note that
I
consists of all tuples
(D_{1},\dots,D_{\ell(\psi)})
such that
D_{j}\subseteq\psi_{j}
is a union of odd many blocks of
\pi
. Then,

|I|=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}\left(\sum_{i\text{ is odd}}\binom{c_{j}}{i}\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\psi)}2^{c_{j}-1}=2^{\ell(\pi)-\ell(\psi)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop{\pi(\phi)\leq\pi,\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)\geq\psi\atop|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|\text{is odd}}}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}=\sum_{D\in I}\sum_{\phi\in I_{D}}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}=2^{\ell(\pi)-\ell(\psi)}(-1)^{\ell(\pi)}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
10.2
.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}_{\pi}=\sum_{\tau\leq\pi}\mathbf{m}_{\tau}=\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}\mathbf{M}_{\phi}.

We show that if there is a block
\pi_{j}
in
\pi
such that
|\pi_{j}|
is even, then

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}(\mathbf{p}_{\pi})=0.

Define

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\imath:&\{\phi\vDash[n]:\pi(\phi)\leq\pi\}&\rightarrow&\{\phi\vDash[n]:\pi(\phi)\leq\pi\}\end{array}

where

\imath(\phi)=\begin{cases}\phi&\text{if~}\phi_{\ell(\phi)}=\pi_{j}\\
\phi_{1}|\phi_{2}|\cdots|\phi_{m-1}|\phi_{m}\cup\phi_{m+1}|\phi_{m+1}|\cdots|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}&\text{if ~}\phi_{m}=\pi_{j},m<\ell(\phi)\\
\phi_{1}|\phi_{2}|\cdots|\phi_{m-1}|\pi_{j}|\phi_{m}\setminus\pi_{j}|\phi_{m+1}|\cdots|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}&\text{if~}\pi_{j}\subsetneq\phi_{m}\text{~for some~}m.\end{cases}

Consider
\{\phi\vDash[n]:\pi(\phi)\leq\pi\}
as a sign set where the sign of
\phi
is
(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}
. Then
\imath
is a sign reversing involution.

By Proposition
9.2
, we have

(10.1)

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{M}_{\phi})=\begin{cases}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)+n}\displaystyle\sum_{\psi\leq\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)}2^{\ell(\psi)}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}&\text{ if $|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|$}\text{~ is odd,}\\
0&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Note that if
\imath(\phi)\neq\phi
and
|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|
is odd, then
|\iota(\phi)_{\ell(\iota(\phi))}|
is odd, moreover,

\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)=\mathrm{Odd}(\imath(\phi))\quad\text{and}\quad(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}=(-1)^{\ell(\imath(\phi))\pm 1}.

Consequently, if
\iota(\phi)\neq\phi
,

\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}({\mathbf{M}}_{\phi})=-\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}({\mathbf{M}}_{\imath(\phi)}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}(\mathbf{p}_{\pi})

\displaystyle=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}\left(\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}\mathbf{M}_{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi,\imath(\phi)=\phi}\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{\phi}\right)=0,

where the last equality is because if
\imath(\phi)=\phi
, then
|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|
is even, and so by Equation
10.1
, we have
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{\phi}\right)=0
.

Assume
\pi
is an odd set partition, then since all blocks of
\pi
have odd sizes,
(-1)^{n}=(-1)^{\ell(\pi)}
. Using Theorem
10.1

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}(\mathbf{p}_{\pi})

\displaystyle=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}\left(\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}\mathbf{M}_{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|\text{~is odd,}\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle(\text{by Equation \ref{eq:theta-M}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|\text{~is odd,}\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}\left((-1)^{\ell(\phi)+n}\displaystyle\sum_{\psi\leq\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)}2^{\ell(\psi)}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle(\text{by reordering the sums})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}\left(2^{\ell(\psi)}(-1)^{\ell(\pi)}\left(\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]:\pi(\phi)\leq\pi\atop\mathrm{Odd}(\phi)\geq\psi,|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}|\text{~is odd}}(-1)^{\ell(\phi)}\right)\right){\mathbf{M}}_{\psi}

\displaystyle(\text{by Lemma \ref{lem:odd-set}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\psi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\psi)\leq\pi}\left(2^{\ell(\psi)}(-1)^{\ell(\pi)}2^{\ell(\pi)-\ell(\psi)}(-1)^{\ell(\pi)}\right){\mathbf{M}}_{\psi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\psi\vDash[n]:\atop\pi(\phi)\leq\pi}2^{\ell(\pi)}{\mathbf{M}}_{\psi}

\displaystyle=2^{\ell(\pi)}\mathbf{p}_{\pi}.

∎

Recall that for a set partition
\pi
, the
Schur
Q
-function in noncommuting variables
is

\mathbf{q}_{\pi}=\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{\pi}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\sigma\circ Q_{\pi}}=\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{\pi}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\sigma\circ P_{\pi}}.

Note that

\mathbf{q}_{\pi}=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{h}_{\pi})=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{e}_{\pi}).

The space of the Schur
Q
-functions is defined to be the image of
\Theta_{\mathrm{Sym}}
, similarly, we define the
space of Schur
Q
-functions in noncommuting variables
,
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
, to be the image of
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}
i.e.,

\mathrm{NC}\Omega=\mathrm{Img}(\mathrm{NCSym}).

The following corollary is a result of Theorem
10.2
.

Corollary 10.4
.

The set
\{p_{\pi}:\pi\vdash[n],\pi\text{ is odd set partition}\}
forms a linear basis of
\mathrm{NC}\Omega_{n}
.

The main goal of this section is to show that

\mathrm{NC}\Omega=\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi

and also

\{\mathbf{q}_{\pi}:\pi\text{~is an odd set partition}\}

is a basis for
\mathrm{NC}\Omega_{n}
, so the dimension of
\mathrm{NC}\Omega_{n}
is equal to the number of odd set partitions.

Recall from Lemma
7.3
that

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{(B,\sigma)}=(-1)^{|B|}\sum_{A\subseteq\mathrm{Odd}(B)}2^{|A|+1}\mathbf{M}_{(A,\sigma)}.

For any odd set composition
\phi
, we have

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\phi}=(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}\sum_{\psi\leq\phi}2^{\ell(\psi)}{\mathbf{M}}_{\psi}

under the identification
{\rm SetCompOdd}:(B,\sigma)\mapsto\phi
.

For any odd set partition
\pi
define

\mathbf{n}_{\pi}=(-1)^{(n-\ell(\pi))/2}\sum_{\pi(\phi)=\pi}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\phi}.

Theorem 10.5
.

We have that

\{\mathbf{n}_{\pi}:\pi\vdash[n],\pi\text{ is an odd set partition}\}

is a basis for
\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi
.

Proof.

Clearly,
\{\mathbf{n}_{\pi}:\pi\vdash[n],\pi\text{ is an odd set partition}\}
is linearly independent and
\mathbf{n}_{\pi}\in\mathrm{NC}\Pi
.

For any odd set composition
\pi\vdash[n]
, we have

\mathbf{n}_{\pi}=(-1)^{(n-\ell(\pi))/2}\sum_{\pi(\phi)=\pi}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\phi}=\sum_{\pi(\phi)=\pi}\sum_{\psi\leq\phi}2^{\ell(\psi)}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}=\sum_{\psi\models[n]}\left(\sum_{\phi\geq\psi\atop\pi(\phi)=\pi}1\right)2^{\ell(\psi)}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}.

Suppose
\pi(\psi)=\pi(\varphi)
. We define a bijection

g:\{\phi\geq\psi:\pi(\phi)=\pi\}\to\{\phi\geq\varphi:\pi(\phi)=\pi\}

as follows: if
\psi
is obtained from
\phi
by replacing some consecutive blocks
\phi_{t}|\phi_{t+1}|\cdots|\phi_{t+k}
of
\phi
by
\psi_{i}
, then since
\psi_{i}=\varphi_{j}
, we obtain
g(\phi)
by replacing
\varphi_{j}
by
\phi_{t}|\phi_{t+1}|\cdots|\phi_{t+k}
. Then
g
is a bijection, and so
\mathbf{n}_{\pi}\in\mathrm{NCSym}
.

Suppose
f\in\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi_{n}
. We show that

f
is a linear combination of the elements in the set
\{\mathbf{n}_{\pi}:\pi~\text{is an odd set partition}\}
.

Let
f=\displaystyle\sum_{\phi\models[n]\atop\phi\text{ is odd }}c_{\phi}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\phi}
. We have

f=\sum_{\phi\models[n]\atop\phi\text{ is odd }}(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}\sum_{\psi\leq\phi}2^{\ell(\psi)}c_{\phi}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}=\sum_{\psi\models[n]}\left(\sum_{\psi\leq\phi\atop\phi\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}c_{\phi}\right)2^{\ell(\psi)}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}.

Since
f\in\mathrm{NCSym}
, we must have

\sum_{\psi\leq\phi\atop\phi\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}c_{\phi}=\sum_{\varphi\leq\phi\atop\phi\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}c_{\phi}

whenever
\pi(\psi)=\pi(\varphi)
. We prove
\pi(\psi)=\pi(\varphi)
implies
c_{\psi}=c_{\varphi}
by induction on
\ell(\psi)
. If
\psi,\varphi\vDash[n]
and
\ell(\psi)=\ell(\varphi)=n
, the equality reduces to
c_{\psi}=c_{\varphi}
and we are done. Assume
\pi(\psi)=\pi(\varphi)
implies
c_{\psi}=c_{\varphi}
for all odd set compositions
\psi,\varphi
with
\ell(\psi)>\ell
. Take odd set compositions
\psi
and
\varphi
with
\pi(\psi)=\pi(\varphi)
and
\ell(\psi)=\ell
. We define a bijection

\mathsf{g}:\{\phi\geq\psi:\phi\text{ is odd}\}\to\{\phi\geq\varphi:\phi\text{ is odd}\}

as follows: if
\psi
is obtained from
\phi
by replacing some consecutive blocks
\phi_{t}|\phi_{t+1}|\cdots|\phi_{t+k}
of
\phi
by
\psi_{i}
, then since
\psi_{i}=\varphi_{j}
, we obtain
\mathsf{g}(\phi)
by replacing
\varphi_{j}
by
\phi_{t}|\phi_{t+1}|\cdots|\phi_{t+k}
. Then
\mathsf{g}
is a bijection. Then,
\pi(\phi)=\pi(\mathsf{g}(\phi))
and by induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle(-1)^{(n-\ell)/2}(c_{\psi}-c_{\varphi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\varphi<\phi\atop\phi\text{ is odd }}(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}c_{\phi}-\sum_{\psi<\phi\atop\phi\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}c_{\phi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\psi<\phi\atop\phi\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{(n-\ell(\phi))/2}(c_{\mathsf{g}(\phi)}-c_{\phi})=0.

Therefore,
\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is spaned by
\{\mathbf{n}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is odd}\}
, and since this is a linearly independent set, we have
\{\mathbf{n}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is odd}\}
is a basis for
\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi
.
∎

Corollary 10.6
.

We have
\mathrm{NC}\Omega=\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi
. In particular,
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
is both a subalgebra and a quotient algebra of
\mathrm{NCSym}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
10.2
,
\mathrm{NC}\Omega\subseteq\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi
. Corollary
10.4
and Theorem
10.5
implies
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
and
\mathrm{NCSym}\cap\mathrm{NC}\Pi
have the same dimension in each degree.
∎

Theorem 10.7
.

We have the following.

(1)

For each set partition
\pi
, we have
\omega(\mathbf{q}_{\pi})=\mathbf{q}_{\pi}
.

(2)

The set
\{\mathbf{q}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is odd set partition}\}
forms a linear basis of
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
.

(3)

As an algebra,
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
is freely generated by the set
\{\mathbf{p}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is connected odd set partition}\}
and the set
\{\mathbf{q}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is connected odd set partition}\}
.

Proof.

(1) Note that

\omega(\mathbf{q}_{\pi})=\omega(\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}({\mathbf{h}}_{\pi}))=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}(\omega({\mathbf{h}}_{\pi}))=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCSym}}({\mathbf{e}}_{\pi})=\mathbf{q}_{\pi}.

(2)
By Theorems
10.2
and
10.1
,

\mathbf{q}_{\pi}=\sum_{\tau\leq\pi\atop\tau\text{ is odd set partition}}|\mu(\hat{0},\tau)|2^{\ell(\tau)}\mathbf{p}_{\tau}.

Observe that for any odd set partition
\tau
,
\mu(\hat{0},\tau)
is always positive. Hence,

\mathbf{q}_{\pi}=\sum_{\tau\leq\pi\atop\tau\text{ is odd }}\mu(\hat{0},\tau)2^{\ell(\tau)}\mathbf{p}_{\tau}=\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}(\mathbf{e}_{\pi}).

By triangularity we have the set
\{\mathbf{q}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is odd}\}
forms a linear basis of
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
.

(3) We have that
\{\mathbf{h}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is connected set partition}\}
generates
\mathrm{NCSym}
. Also,
\mathbf{h}_{\pi}\cdot\mathbf{h}_{\tau}=\mathbf{h}_{\pi|\tau}
. Note that
\pi|\tau
is an odd set partition if and only if
\pi
and
\tau
are odd set partitions. Consequently, the set
\{\mathbf{h}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is odd set partition}\}
can be generated by
\{\mathbf{h}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is connected odd set partition}\}
. Since
\Theta_{\mathrm{NCQSym}}
is an algebra epimorphism, the set
\{\mathbf{q}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is connected odd set partition}\}
generates
\{\mathbf{q}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is odd set partition}\}
and
\mathrm{NC}\Omega
.

The set
\{\mathbf{q}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is connected odd set partition}\}
must be algebraically independent because
\{\mathbf{p}_{\pi}:\pi\text{ is connected odd set partition}\}
is algebraically independent and these two sets have the same cardinality in every degree. This proves (4).
∎

Remark 10.8
.

The commutative version of Theorem
10.7
are classical results that can be found in section 3.8 of
[
33
]
.

11.
Combinatorial identities and conjectures

This section is a preparation for our main result on internal coproduct formula, and it has its own combinatorial interests.

Suppose
\phi=\phi_{1}|\phi_{2}|\cdots|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}
and
\psi=\psi_{1}|\psi_{2}|\cdots|\psi_{\ell(\psi)}
. Let
\phi\land\psi
be the set composition obtained from
(\phi_{1}\cap\psi_{1},\phi_{1}\cap\psi_{2},\dots,\phi_{1}\cap\psi_{\ell(\psi)},\phi_{2}\cap\psi_{1},\dots,\phi_{\ell(\phi)}\cap\psi_{\ell(\psi)})
with empty blocks removed.
For two set compositions
\phi,\psi\models[n]
, recall the
refinement order
is given by
\psi\leq\phi
if the blocks of
\psi
are union of consecutive blocks of
\phi
.

We now consider the sub-poset
\{\phi\models[n]:\phi\text{ is odd set composition}\}
, with inherited refinement order
\leq
. In this section, for odd set compositions
\phi,\psi\models[n]
, we let
\mu(\phi,\psi)
denote the Möbius function of
(\phi,\psi)
on this sub-poset.

Lemma 11.1
.

If
\phi\vDash[n]
is an odd set composition, then the sub-posets
P=\{\psi\leq\phi:\psi\text{ is odd set composition}\}
is isomorphic to the poset
P^{\prime}=\{\alpha\vDash\ell(\phi):\alpha\text{ is odd composition}\}
.

Proof.

We construct a map
f:P\to P^{\prime}
such that
\ell(\psi)=\ell(f(\psi))
recursively as follows. First, define
f(\phi)=\hat{1}=(1,1,\dots,1)
. Suppose we have already defined
f(\psi)=\alpha
with
\psi=\psi_{1}|\psi_{2}|\cdots|\psi_{\ell(\psi)}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\cdots\alpha_{\ell(\psi)}
. If
\varphi\in P
is obtained from
\psi
by merging
\psi_{i},\psi_{i+1},\psi_{i+2}
, then we define
f(\varphi)
to be obtained from
\alpha
by merging
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i+1},\alpha_{i+2}
. Then
f(\varphi)\in P^{\prime}
and
\ell(\psi)=\ell(f(\psi))
. Clearly,
f
is a bijection that preserves the partial order.
∎

From the above lemma, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{\psi\leq\phi\atop\psi\text{ is odd}}\mu(\psi,\phi)

depends only on the length of
\phi
. We define
\mu_{\ell}
to be the sum above for any
\phi
with
\ell(\phi)=\ell
.
If
\ell
is odd, then there is a unique minimum element among the summands, namely
\{[\ell]\}
. Hence,
\mu_{1}=1
and
\mu_{\ell}=0
for
\ell>1
. For any odd composition
\beta\leq\alpha
, it is also consistent to write
\mu(\beta,\alpha)=\mu(\psi,\phi)
when
\alpha(\psi)=\beta
,
\alpha(\phi)=\alpha
and
\psi\leq\phi
.

Remark 11.2
.

It has been shown in
[
19
, Corollary 1.4 and Theorem 2.5]
that
\mu(\beta,\alpha)
and
\mu_{\ell}
are signed Catalan numbers. Let
\mathrm{Cat}_{n}=\frac{1}{n+1}\binom{2n}{n}
be the
n
-th Catalan number. If
\beta,\alpha
are odd compositions and
\beta\leq\alpha
, then

\mu(\beta,\alpha)=(-1)^{(\ell(\alpha)-\ell(\beta))/2}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\ell(\alpha)-\ell(\beta))/2}.

Moreover, if
\ell
is even, then
\mu_{\ell}=(-1)^{\ell/2-1}\mathrm{Cat}_{\ell/2-1}
.

Lemma 11.3
.

For any
n
, we have

\sum_{\alpha\vDash n\atop\alpha\text{ is odd}}\mu_{\ell(\alpha)}=1.

Proof.

By definition, we can rewrite the left-hand side as

\sum_{\alpha\vDash n\atop\alpha\text{ is odd}}\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha\atop\beta\text{ is odd}}\mu(\beta,\alpha)=\sum_{\beta\vDash n\atop\beta\text{ is odd}}\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha\atop\alpha\text{ is odd}}\mu(\beta,\alpha).

Since there is a unique maximal element in the poset of odd compositions of
n
, namely
\hat{1}=(1,1,\dots,1)
, for any
\beta\neq\hat{1}
,

\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha\atop\beta\text{ is odd}}\mu(\beta,\alpha)=0.

Hence, the expression can be further reduced to
\mu(\hat{1},\hat{1})=1
.
∎

For a set composition
\phi=\phi_{1}|\phi_{2}|\cdots|\phi_{\ell(\phi)}
and a set composition
\psi\leq\phi
, we define

\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}=\{\phi_{\tau(1)}|\phi_{\tau(2)}|\cdots|\phi_{\tau(\ell(\phi))}:\tau\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell(\phi)},\psi\land\left(\phi_{\tau(1)}|\phi_{\tau(2)}|\cdots|\phi_{\tau(\ell(\phi))}\right)=\phi\}.

For example, if
\phi=3|1|4|2
and
\psi=3|124
, then
\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}=\{3|1|4|2,1|3|4|2,1|4|3|2,1|4|2|3\}
.

Proposition 11.4
.

Let
\phi
be a set composition and
\psi\leq\phi
. Then for a set compostition
\varphi
, we have
\psi\leq\psi\land\varphi\leq\phi
if and only if
\varphi\leq\xi
for some
\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
.

Proof.

The fact that
\psi\leq\psi\land\varphi
is obvious. Let
\phi=\phi_{1}|\phi_{2}|\cdots|\phi_{\ell}(\phi)
,
\psi\land\varphi=C_{1}|C_{2}|\cdots|C_{k}
and
\varphi=\varphi_{1}|\varphi_{2}|\cdots|\varphi_{\ell(\varphi)}
.

If
\psi\land\varphi\leq\phi
, then each block
\phi_{i}
of
\phi
is contained in some block
C_{j}
of
\psi\land\varphi
which is contained in some block
\psi_{m}
of
\varphi
. Let
\xi\geq\varphi
obtained from
\varphi
by decomposing each block
\psi_{m}
into
\phi_{m_{1}}|\phi_{m_{2}}|\cdots|\phi_{m_{q}}
such that
m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{q}
.

Then,
\psi\land\xi
is obtained by decomposing each block
C_{j}=A\cap\psi_{m}
for some block
A
of
\psi
into
A\cap\phi_{m_{1}}|A\cap\phi_{m_{2}}|\cdots|A\cap\phi_{m_{q}}
. Each block
A\cap\phi_{m_{i}}
is either
\phi_{m_{i}}
or
\emptyset
.

Assume that
\psi\land\xi=\phi_{\tau(1)}|\phi_{\tau(2)}|\cdots|\phi_{\tau(\ell(\phi))}.

•

If
\phi_{\tau(i)}\subseteq C_{j}
,
\phi_{\tau(i+1)}\subseteq C_{j+1}
for some
j
, then
\tau(i)<\tau(i+1)
since
\psi\land\varphi\leq\phi
.

•

If
\phi_{\tau(i)},\phi_{\tau(i+1)}\subset C_{j}
for some
j
, then
\tau(i)=m_{s}
,
\tau(i+1)=m_{t}
where
\phi_{m_{s}},\phi_{m_{t}}\subset\psi_{m}
for some
m
and
s<t
. By construction,
m_{s}<m_{t}
and
\tau(i)<\tau(i+1)
.

Therefore,
\tau
is the identity permutation and
\psi\land\xi=\phi
, and so
\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
.

The converse can be seen from covering relations. If
\varphi
is obtained from
\varphi^{\prime}
by merging two blocks
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
of
\varphi^{\prime}
, then
\psi\land\varphi
is obtained from
\psi\land\varphi^{\prime}
by merging
A\cap A_{1}
and
A\cap A_{2}
for each block
A
of
\psi
. Hence,
\varphi\leq\xi
implies
\psi\land\varphi\leq\psi\land\xi=\phi
.
∎

Let

\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}=\{\chi\leq\xi:\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi},\chi\text{ is odd set composition}\}=\{\chi:\psi\land\chi\leq\phi,\chi\text{ is odd set composition}\}.

For example, take
\phi=3|1|4|2
and
\psi=3|124
, then
\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}=\{3|1|4|2,1|3|4|2,1|4|3|2,1|4|2|3\}
and
\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}=\{3|1|4|2,1|3|4|2,1|4|3|2,1|4|2|3,134|2,3|124,1|234,124|3\}
.

The following theorem is the most technical part of this section.

Theorem 11.5
.

Let
\phi\vDash[n]
be an odd set composition and
\psi\leq\phi
. Then,

(11.1)

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}}2^{\ell(\psi\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\mu_{\ell(\chi)}=2^{\ell(\psi)-1}.

Proof.

First observe that for any set composition appearing on the left-hand side, each of its blocks is a union of blocks of
\phi
. Therefore, the exact contents of the blocks in
\phi
play no role, as long as the sizes are odd. We only need to prove the case
\phi=1|2|\cdots|n
.

If
n
is odd, then
\ell(\chi)
is odd for all
\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}
. Hence,
\mu_{\ell(\chi)}=0
unless
\chi=\{[n]\}
. The equality holds since
\psi\land\{[n]\}=\psi
.

Assume
n
is even. We begin with a special case. If
\psi=\{[n]\}
, then
\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}=\{\phi\}
and the poset
\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}
is isomorphic to the poset
\{\alpha\vDash n:\alpha\text{ is odd composition}\}
. Furthermore,
\psi\land\chi=\chi
for all
\chi\vDash[n]
. Hence, the equality holds by Lemma
11.3
.

We prove Equation
11.1
by induction on
n
. The base case
n=2
can be easily checked and we omit it. Assume the equality holds for
n<\ell
. Let
\phi=1|2|\cdots|\ell
.

Let
\psi=\psi_{1}|\psi_{2}|\cdots|\psi_{\ell(\psi)}
with
\psi_{i}=\{j+1,j+2,\dots,j+m\}
and
\psi_{i+1}=\{j+m+1\}
, and let
\psi^{\prime}=\psi_{1}|\cdots|\psi_{i-1}|\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}|\psi_{i+2}|\cdots|\psi_{\ell(\psi)}
. That is,
\psi^{\prime}
is obtained from
\psi
by merging a block of size
m
and a block of size
1
. Then, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sum_{{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}}}2^{\ell(\psi\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\mu_{\ell(\chi)}=2^{\ell(\psi)-1}\text{~if and only if~}\displaystyle\sum_{{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}}2^{\ell(\psi^{\prime}\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\mu_{\ell(\chi)}=2^{\ell(\psi^{\prime})-1}

since all set compositions
\varphi\leq\phi
can be obtained by a series of such processes from the base case
\{[n]\}
.

Suppose
\chi=\chi_{1}|\cdots|\chi_{\ell(\chi)}\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell(\chi)}
. We now define
\sigma(\chi)
to be the set composition obtained from
\chi_{\sigma(1)}|\cdots|\chi_{\sigma({\ell(\chi)})}
by
renumbering
each block
\psi_{r}
of
\psi
increasingly, that is, if
\psi_{r}=\{a_{1}<\cdots<a_{p}\}
and
\chi_{\sigma(1)}|\cdots|\chi_{\sigma({\ell(\chi)})}=\mathrm{SetComp}(A,\tau)
, then
\sigma(\chi)=\mathrm{SetComp}(A,\tau^{\prime})
where
\tau^{\prime}
is the permutation with
\tau^{\prime-1}(a_{1})<\cdots<\tau^{\prime-1}(a_{p})
, and
\tau^{-1}(q)=\tau^{\prime-1}(q)
for
q\notin\psi_{r}
. For example, if
\psi=146|35|2
,
\chi=134|256
and
\sigma=21
, then
\chi_{\sigma(1)}|\chi_{\sigma(2)}=256|134
, and we need to renumber
\{1,4,6\}
and
\{3,5\}
increasingly. Hence,
\sigma(\chi)=123|456
.

Clearly,
\sigma(\chi)
is an odd set composition. The renumbering ensures that
\psi\land\sigma(\chi)\leq\phi
. Therefore,
\sigma(\chi)\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}
. Moreover,
\ell(\psi\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)=\ell(\psi\land\sigma(\chi))-\ell(\sigma(\chi))
because

\ell(\psi\land\chi)=\sum_{1\leq p\leq{\ell(\chi)}}|\{1\leq r\leq{\ell(\psi)}:\chi_{p}\cap\psi_{r}\neq\emptyset\}|

and
\{1\leq r\leq{\ell(\psi)}:\chi_{p}\cap\psi_{r}\neq\emptyset\}=\{1\leq r\leq{\ell(\psi)}:\chi_{\sigma(p)}\cap\psi_{r}\neq\emptyset\}
. For simplicity, we write
c_{\chi}^{\psi}=2^{\ell(\psi\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\mu_{\ell(\chi)}
. Then we have
c_{\chi}^{\psi}=c_{\sigma(\chi)}^{\psi}
.

We partition
\{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}\}
into classes according to
\chi|_{\psi_{i}}
and
\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}
. For any
1\leq t\leq m
, let
A^{t}=\{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}:\ell(\chi|_{\psi_{i}})=\ell(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}})-1=t\}
and
B^{t}=\{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}:\ell(\chi|_{\psi_{i}})=\ell(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}})=t\}
.

Assume
\chi=\chi_{1}|\cdots|\chi_{\ell(\chi)}\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}
and let
\{1\leq p\leq{\ell(\chi)}:\chi_{p}\cap\psi_{i}\neq\emptyset\}=\{p_{1}<\dots<p_{t}\}
. If
\chi\in A^{t}
, let
\psi_{i+1}\subseteq\chi_{p_{t+1}}
, then there are
t+1
permutations in the subgroup of
\mathfrak{S}_{\ell(\chi)}
generated by the
(t+1)
-cycle
(p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{t+1})
. Moreover, for each such permutation
\sigma
, we have
c_{\sigma(\chi)}^{\psi}=c_{\chi}^{\psi}=c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}
. Among these cyclic permutations, there is a unique permutation
\sigma
making
\sigma(p_{t+1})=\max\{p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{t+1}\}
, in which case
\sigma(\chi)\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in A^{t}}c_{\chi}^{\psi}=(t+1)\sum_{\chi\in A^{t}\cap\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

Similarly, if
\chi\in B^{t}
, then there are
t
permutations in the subgroup of
\mathfrak{S}_{\ell(\chi)}
generated by the
t
-cycle
(p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{t})
. Moreover, for each such permutation
\sigma
, we have
c_{\sigma(\chi)}^{\psi}=c_{\chi}^{\psi}=2c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}
. Among these cyclic permutaitons, there is a unique permutation
\sigma
making
\psi_{i+1}\subseteq\chi_{p_{t}}
, in which case
\sigma(\chi)\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in B^{t}}c_{\chi}^{\psi}=2t\sum_{\chi\in B^{t}\cap\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

Combining these two cases yields

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}}c_{\chi}^{\psi}=\sum_{\alpha\vDash m}\left((\ell(\alpha)+1)\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\alpha\cdot 1}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}+2\ell(\alpha)\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\alpha\odot 1}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\right).

Note that for
\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}
, the order of appearance of
\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}
is fixed. Hence,

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}=\sum_{\alpha\vDash m}\left(\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\alpha\cdot 1}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}+\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\alpha\odot 1}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\right).

If
m=1
, then
\{\alpha\vDash m\}=\{1\}
and

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}}c_{\chi}^{\psi}=2\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}},

so we are done.

Assume that
m\geq 2
, let
\phi^{\prime}
be obtained from
\phi
by merging
\{j+1\},\{j+2\}
, and
\{j+3\}
. We use again the fact that the contents of blocks in
\phi^{\prime}
play no role and the induction hypothesis to get

\sum_{\alpha\vDash m-1}\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=2\odot\alpha}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}=\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi^{\prime}}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}=2^{\ell(\psi^{\prime})-1}.

We now claim that

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}}c_{\chi}^{\psi}+(m-1)\left(\sum_{\alpha\vDash m-1}\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=2\odot\alpha}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\right)=(m+1)\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

If the claim were true, we have

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}}c_{\chi}^{\psi}+(m-1)2^{\ell(\psi^{\prime})-1}=(m+1)\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

It is clear that

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi}}c_{\chi}^{\psi}=2^{\ell(\psi)-1}\text{~if and only if~}\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}=2^{\ell(\psi^{\prime})-1}

and we are done.

To prove our claim, we define an equivalence relation on
\{\alpha\vDash m+1\}\cup\{\alpha\vDash m-1\}
by
\alpha
is equivalent to
\beta=(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{t})
if

(1)

\alpha=(\beta_{\sigma(1)},\dots,\beta_{\sigma(t)})
for some
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{t}
, or

(2)

\beta\vDash m-1
and
\alpha=(\beta_{1}+2,\beta_{2},\dots,\beta_{t})
,

and take the transitive closure. For example, take
m=6
, then the equivalence class containing
32
is
\{32,23,52,25,43,34,41,14,61,16\}
. Equivalently, suppose
\alpha
consists of
a
odd parts and
b
even parts, then the equivalence class of
\alpha
is the set of
\beta\vDash m+1
or
m-1
that consists of
a
odd parts and
b
even parts. We use
E(a,b)
to denote such an equivalence class.

If
\alpha\vDash m+1
is equivalent to
\beta\vDash m+1
satisfying (1), then by the group action,

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\alpha}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}=\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\beta}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

If
\alpha\vDash m-1
is equivalent to
\beta\vDash m-1
satisfying (1), we claim

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=2\odot\alpha}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}=\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=2\odot\beta}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

For all compositions
\chi
on both sides,
j+1,j+2
and
j+3
appear in the same block of
\chi
. Hence, we can view
\{j+1,j+2,j+3\}
as a single element and apply the group action.

If
\alpha\vDash m+1
is equivalent to
\beta\vDash m-1
satisfying (2), then obviously

\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\alpha}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}=\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=2\odot\beta}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

For each equivalence class
E(a,b)
, let
\alpha\in E(a,b)
with
\alpha\vDash m+1
, and let

c(a,b)=\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathscr{D}_{\phi}^{\psi^{\prime}}\atop\alpha\left(\chi|_{\psi_{i}\cup\psi_{i+1}}\right)=\alpha}c_{\chi}^{\psi^{\prime}}.

We examine the coefficients of
c(a,b)
on both sides of the equation.

On the left-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b}\left(\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b),\alpha\vDash m+1\atop\alpha_{a+b}=1}(a+b)+\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b),\alpha\vDash m+1\atop\alpha_{a+b}>1}2(a+b)+\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b)\atop\alpha\vDash m-1}(m-1)\right)c(a,b)

and on the right-hand side, we have

\sum_{a,b}\left(\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b)\atop\alpha\vDash m+1}(m+1)\right)c(a,b).

Recall that the number of compositions of
n
into
a
odd parts and
b
even parts is
\displaystyle\binom{\frac{n+a-2}{2}}{a+b-1}\binom{a+b}{a}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b),\alpha\vDash m+1\atop\alpha_{a+b}=1}(a+b)+\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b),\alpha\vDash m+1\atop\alpha_{a+b}>1}2(a+b)+\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b)\atop\alpha\vDash m-1}(m-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b)\atop\alpha\vDash m+1}2(a+b)-\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b),\alpha\vDash m+1\atop\alpha_{a+b}=1}(a+b)+\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b)\atop\alpha\vDash m-1}(m-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{\frac{m+a-1}{2}}{a+b-1}\binom{a+b}{a}2(a+b)-\binom{\frac{m+a-3}{2}}{a+b-2}\binom{a+b-1}{a-1}(a+b)+\binom{\frac{m+a-3}{2}}{a+b-1}\binom{a+b}{a}(m-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{\frac{m+a-1}{2}}{a+b-1}\binom{a+b}{a}\left(2(a+b)-\frac{2a(a+b-1)}{m+a-1}+\frac{(m-a-2b+1)(m-1)}{m+a-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{\frac{m+a-1}{2}}{a+b-1}\binom{a+b}{a}(m+1)=\sum_{\alpha\in E(a,b)\atop\alpha\vDash m+1}(m+1).

∎

From now on for any odd set composition
\phi\vDash[n]
, let
p(\phi)=(n-\ell(\phi))/2
.

Let
\phi
be an odd set composition and
\psi\leq\phi
. For each odd set composition
\varphi
such that
\varphi\leq\xi
for some
\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
, we define the coefficient
C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}
recursively as follows:

\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{p(\zeta)}C_{\zeta}^{\phi\psi}=(-1)^{p(\phi)}2^{\ell(\psi\land\varphi)-\ell(\varphi)}.

By inclusion-exclusion, we can see that

C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}=(-1)^{p(\varphi)+p(\phi)}\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}\mu(\varphi,\zeta)2^{\ell(\psi\land\zeta)-\ell(\zeta)}.

In particular,
\varphi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
, implies
C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}=1
.

Corollary 11.6
.

Let
\phi
be an odd set composition and
\psi\leq\phi
. Then for any set composition
\varphi
, not necessarily odd, such that
\varphi\leq\xi
for some
\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
, we have

\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{p(\zeta)}C_{\zeta}^{\phi\psi}=(-1)^{p(\phi)}2^{\ell(\psi\land\varphi)-\ell(\varphi)}.

Proof.

For simplicity, we write
\mathscr{C}=\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
and
C_{\zeta}=C_{\zeta}^{\phi\psi}
. Assume
\varphi\leq\xi
for some
\xi\in\mathscr{C}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop\zeta\text{ is odd }}(-1)^{p(\zeta)}C_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{p(\phi)}\left(\sum_{\zeta\leq\chi\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop\chi\text{ is odd}}\mu(\zeta,\chi)2^{\ell(\psi\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p(\phi)}\sum_{\varphi\leq\chi\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop\chi\text{ is odd}}2^{\ell(\psi\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\left(\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\chi\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}\mu(\zeta,\chi)\right).

This can be computed on each block of
\varphi
separately. Let
\varphi=\varphi_{1}|\varphi_{2}|\cdots|\varphi_{\ell(\varphi)}\vDash[n]
and
\psi=\psi_{1}|\psi_{2}|\cdots|\psi_{k}\vDash[n]
. When
\varphi\leq\chi\leq\xi
for some
\xi\in\mathscr{C}
and
\chi
is odd set composition, then
\chi=\chi_{1}|\cdots|\chi_{\ell(\chi)}
such that for all
j
,
\varphi_{j}=\chi_{i_{j}+1}\cup\chi_{i_{j}+2}\cup\cdots\cup\chi_{i_{j+1}}
for some
i_{j},i_{j+1}
. We have the following.

•

Let
a_{i}=\ell(\psi|_{\chi_{i}})
. Then,

\displaystyle\ell(\psi\land\chi)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell(\chi)}a_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell(\varphi)}\sum_{i=i_{j}+1}^{i_{j+1}}a_{i}.

•

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\chi\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}\mu(\zeta,\chi)=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\varphi)}\sum_{\beta\vDash i_{j+1}-i_{j}\atop\beta\text{ is odd composition}}\mu(\beta,\hat{1})

where
\hat{1}=(1,1,\dots,1)
is the maximal composition.

Now, let
\phi_{j}=\mathrm{std}(\phi|_{\varphi_{j}})
and
\psi_{j}=\mathrm{std}(\psi|_{\varphi_{j}})
where
\mathrm{std}(-)
gives the unique set composition that preserves the relative order of elements in the blocks. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi\leq\chi\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop\chi\text{ is odd}}2^{\ell(\psi\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\left(\sum_{\varphi\leq\zeta\leq\chi\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}\mu(\zeta,\chi)\right)=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\varphi)}\left(\sum_{\chi\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi_{j}}^{\psi_{j}}\atop\chi\text{ is odd}}2^{\ell(\psi_{j}\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\left(\sum_{\zeta\leq\chi\atop\zeta\text{ is odd}}\mu(\zeta,\chi)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\varphi)}2^{\ell(\psi_{j})-1}=2^{\ell(\psi\land\varphi)-\ell(\varphi)}.

The second equality follows from Lemma
11.5
.
∎

In our computation, we find the following identity that relates Catalan numbers with Eulerian numbers that we cannot prove. This problem is irrelevant to the main result of this section.

Conjecture 11.7
.

If
n
is odd, then

\sum_{\phi\vDash[n]\atop\phi\text{ is odd}}2^{n-\ell(\phi)}\mu_{\ell(\phi)+1}=\sum_{0\leq k\leq n-1}(-1)^{k}E(n,k)

where
E(n,k)
are the Eulerian numbers i.e. number of permutations of
n
with
k
descents. (OEIS A000182 with alternating sign)

Remark 11.8
.

Let
\phi
be an odd set composition and
\psi\leq\phi
. Let
\varphi
be a set composition such that
\varphi\leq\xi
for some
\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
. Let
\varphi=\varphi_{1}|\varphi_{2}|\cdots|\varphi_{\ell(\varphi)}\vDash[n]
and
\psi=\psi_{1}|\psi_{2}|\cdots|\psi_{k}\vDash[n]
. When
\varphi\leq\chi\leq\xi
for some
\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
and
\chi
is odd set composition, then
\chi=\chi_{1}|\cdots|\chi_{\ell(\chi)}
such that for all
j
,
\varphi_{j}=\chi_{i_{j}+1}\cup\chi_{i_{j}+2}\cup\cdots\cup\chi_{i_{j+1}}
for some
i_{j},i_{j+1}
.
We state without proof that

C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}=(-1)^{p(\phi)+p(\varphi)}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell(\varphi)}\left(\sum_{\chi\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi_{j}}^{\psi_{j}}\atop\chi\text{ is odd}}2^{\ell(\psi_{j}\land\chi)-\ell(\chi)}\mu_{\ell(\chi)+1}\right).

If every block of
\varphi
is contained in some block of
\psi
, then
\ell(\psi\land\zeta)=\ell(\zeta)
for all
\zeta\geq\varphi
. In this case, it can be deduced that
C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}=0
unless
\varphi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
. If Problem
11.7
is true, then
\psi=\phi
implies
C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}
is non-negative. A cancellation-free formula for
C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}
remains open.

Conjecture 11.9
.

The coefficients
C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}
are non-negative.

By taking the special case of
\psi=\phi
in Lemma
11.5
, we have that
if the Conjecture
11.7
is true and if
n
is even, then

\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\binom{n}{2k-1}2^{2k-2}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{2k-1}(-1)^{m}E(2k-1,m)\right)=2^{n-1}.

12.
The peak algebra as a left co-ideal of
\mathrm{NCQSym}
under the internal coproduct

The goal of this section is to prove that
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
is a left co-ideal of
\mathrm{NCQSym}
under the internal coproduct.
In NCQSym, one can define (cf.
[
8
]
) an internal coproduct
\Delta^{\circ}:\mathrm{NCQSym}\to\mathrm{NCQSym}\otimes\mathrm{NCQSym}
via the substitution
f(\mathbf{x})\mapsto f(\mathbf{xy})\mapsto f(\mathbf{x})\otimes f(\mathbf{y})
with
\{\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{j}\}
ordered lexicographically. Together with the regular product,
m
, of power series,
(\mathrm{NCQSym},m,\Delta^{\circ})
forms an ungraded bialgebra.

The internal coproduct is given by

\Delta^{\circ}(\mathbf{M}_{\phi})=\sum_{\psi\land\varphi=\phi}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}\otimes\mathbf{M}_{\varphi}.

For example,
\Delta^{\circ}(\mathbf{M}_{1|2})=\mathbf{M}_{12}\otimes\mathbf{M}_{1|2}+\mathbf{M}_{1|2}\otimes(\mathbf{M}_{12}+\mathbf{M}_{1|2}+\mathbf{M}_{2|1})
. Note that
\varphi\land\psi\neq\psi\land\varphi
in general, so
\Delta^{\circ}
is not cocommutative. Since
\psi\land\varphi=\phi
implies
\psi\leq\phi
, we will use the following refined formula

\Delta^{\circ}(\mathbf{M}_{\phi})=\sum_{\psi\leq\phi\atop\psi\land\varphi=\phi}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}\otimes\mathbf{M}_{\varphi}.

We are ready to prove the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 12.1
.

Let
\phi
be an odd set composition. We have that

\Delta^{\circ}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\phi})=\sum_{\psi\leq\phi}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}\otimes\left(\sum_{\varphi\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}\atop\varphi\text{ is odd }}C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\varphi}\right)

In particular, the set
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
forms a left co-ideal in
\mathrm{NCQSym}
under the internal coproduct i.e.
\Delta^{\circ}(\mathrm{NC}\Pi)\subseteq\mathrm{NCQSym}\otimes\mathrm{NC}\Pi
.

Proof.

Fix an odd set composition
\phi
, Recall that
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\phi}=(-1)^{p(\phi)}\sum_{\chi\leq\phi}2^{\ell(\chi)}\mathbf{M}_{\chi}
. We have that

\displaystyle\Delta^{\circ}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\phi})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p(\phi)}\sum_{\chi\leq\phi}2^{\ell(\chi)}\Delta^{\circ}(\mathbf{M}_{\chi})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p(\phi)}\sum_{\chi\leq\phi}2^{\ell(\chi)}\left(\sum_{\psi\leq\chi\atop\psi\land\epsilon=\chi}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}\otimes\mathbf{M}_{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\psi\leq\phi}\mathbf{M}_{\psi}\otimes\left((-1)^{p(\phi)}\sum_{\psi\leq\chi\leq\phi\atop\psi\land\epsilon=\chi}2^{\ell(\chi)}\mathbf{M}_{\epsilon}\right)

Now we fix
\psi\leq\phi
and for simplicity, we write
\mathscr{C}=\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}
and
C_{\varphi}=C_{\varphi}^{\phi\psi}
. By Lemma
11.4
and Corollary
11.6
,

\displaystyle(-1)^{p(\phi)}\sum_{\psi\leq\chi\leq\phi\atop\psi\land\varphi=\chi}2^{\ell(\chi)}\mathbf{M}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p(\phi)}\sum_{\epsilon\leq\xi~\text{for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}}2^{\ell(\psi\land\epsilon)}\mathbf{M}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\epsilon\leq\xi~\text{for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}}\left(2^{\ell(\epsilon)}\sum_{\varphi\leq{\varphi}\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop{\varphi}\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{p({\varphi})}C_{\varphi}\right)\mathbf{M}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\varphi}\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop{\varphi}\text{ is odd}}(-1)^{p(\varphi)}C_{\varphi}\left(\sum_{\epsilon\leq{\varphi}}2^{\ell(\epsilon)}\mathbf{M}_{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\varphi}\leq\xi\text{ for some }\xi\in\mathscr{C}\atop{\varphi}\text{ is odd}}C_{\varphi}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\varphi}.

∎

Example 12.2
.

Suppose
\phi=3|1|4|2
and
\psi=3|124
, then
\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}=\{3|1|4|2,1|3|4|2,1|4|3|2,1|4|2|3\}
. For
\zeta=134|2
, since
3|124\land 134|2=3|14|2
and
|\{\xi\in\mathscr{C}_{\phi}^{\psi}:\zeta\leq\xi\}|=3
, we have
3-C_{\zeta}^{\phi\psi}=2^{\ell(3|14|2)-\ell(3|124)}
i.e.
C_{\zeta}^{\phi\psi}=1
. Similarly,
C_{124|3}^{\phi\psi}=0
,
C_{3|124}^{\phi\psi}=0
and
C_{1|234}^{\phi\psi}=1
. Hence,

\Delta^{\circ}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{3|1|4|2})=\mathbf{M}_{3|124}\otimes({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{3|1|4|2}+{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{1|3|4|2}+{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{1|4|3|2}+{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{1|4|2|3}+{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{134|2}+{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{1|234})+\cdots.

Remark 12.3
.

The set
\mathrm{NC}\Pi
fails to be a right co-ideal in
\mathrm{NCQSym}
. For example,

\Delta^{\circ}(\mathbf{K}_{(\emptyset,12)})=\mathbf{K}_{(\emptyset,12)}\otimes\mathbf{M}_{12}+4(\mathbf{M}_{12}+\mathbf{M}_{1|2})\otimes\mathbf{M}_{1|2}+4\mathbf{M}_{1|2}\otimes\mathbf{M}_{2|1}

while
\mathbf{M}_{1|2}\notin\mathrm{NC}\Pi
.
